Document 007: 10月通胀数据点评：PPI同比再创新高，结构有什么新的变化？

### Page 1

研究发展部
李席丰
lixf@cspengyuan.com
主要内容：
10月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，低于市场预期（0.8%），低于前值（0.9%）；
10月 PPI同比增长 5.7%，高于市场预期（5.4%），高于前值（5.0%）。
一、CPI：食品项拖累加大，对冲能源成本的上升
（1）食品项同比跌幅扩大，鲜菜鲜果和蛋类环比同步转跌，拖累加大，秋季
供应增加和天气改善是主因。猪价受限于猪周期磨底及需求修复的滞后，短
期难以转为 CPI 的正向拉动。
（2）非食品中，主要支撑力量仍来自高油价和 AI 涨价两大外生性输入变量，
内生需求延续低迷。服务价格环比转涨，来自季节性出行需求和政府主导的
医疗调价。家电、汽车等商品竞争激烈，价格以下跌为主，耐用消费品需求亟
待提振。黄金同比涨幅收窄，环比转为负向拖累。
（3）10 月终端商品与服务需求偏弱，基本对冲上游能源成本向下传导的动
能，带动 CPI 同比回落。需求端，短期制约有国补退坡、能源成本压力上升
等因素，中长期面临居民预期低迷和资产负债表受损带来的“主动去杠杆”。

成本端传导并非普涨，而是延续结构性分化。
二、PPI：结构上出现三个变化
（1）10月 PPI同比上涨 5.7%，较前值上升 0.7pct，创近四年新高，再次强于
市场预期；环比连续三个月上涨，涨幅较上月回落 0.5pct 至 0.7%。
（2）PPI 同比涨幅扩大，原油、煤炭和 AI 延续涨价主线，其中煤炭、石油相
关的能源化工链条对 PPI 同比的拉动由 2.8pct 扩大至 3.7pct；剔除能源链条
后，PPI同比由 2.2%回落至 2.0%。
（3）相比前月，PPI结构上出现三个变化：一是，油气链条的边际贡献由上
游转移到中下游；二是建材链条结构分化，形成地产拖累、基建托底；三是 AI
涨价持续，但是价格弹性收窄。
（4）利润层面，行业利润传导的裂口加大。PPI 采掘业（27.9%）→原材料
PPI 同比再创新高，结构有什么新的变化？
——10月通胀数据点评 宏观研究
2026年 11月 9 日

（11.6%）→加工（3.5%）→生活资料（-0.1%），采掘业到原材料增速差连续
9个月扩大，原材料到加工环节的增速差连续 3个月扩大，加工到生活资料的
增速差相对稳定。
三、展望：核心 CPI 存回落压力，PPI 同比 2027年中枢回落，二季度或加速
下跌
（1）通胀趋势：对于 CPI，PPI 的结构性上涨对 CPI 的拉动有限，同时在
实体预期低迷、总需求没有明显改善的背景下，后续 CPI 同比预计在 0-
1%区间运行，核心 CPI 存回落压力。对于 PPI，预计 11-12 月 PPI 同比在
5%左右的高位，具体增幅主要取决于油价中枢，但 PPI 环比涨速最快的
时间或已经过去。2027年一季度开始，PPI 面临持续上升的基数，价格中
枢预计震荡回落，二季度回落速度或加快。
（2）一揽子增量政策效果观察：资金转化存堵点和时滞，实物工作量形
成较慢，对建材链条终端价格的提振效果有限。总结看，可能来自两方面
因素：一是项目端的施工进度成为主要约束，堵点正从“资金下达”转向
“实物转化”；二是政策资金的投向不偏向传统建材，新兴产业单位投资

用钢用水泥强度远低于传统“铁公基”。专项债和资本金等政策资金的实
物转化时间不同。
（3）对债市的影响：短期信用债赎回风险快速修复，半年维度有经济压
力增加、资金利率中枢下移、长端债券供给减少、股市长周期调整等因素
支撑，四季度债市可能出现“抢跑”行情。30 年国债收益率按照预期向
2.0%靠近，未来一年有望到 1.9%。股市阶段底部较明确，11月迎周度级
别反弹，会修复短期风险偏好。

### Page 3

10 月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，低于市场预期（0.8%），低于前值（0.9%）；
10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，高于市场预期（5.4%），高于前值（5.0%）。
一、CPI：食品项拖累加大，对冲能源成本的上升
10 月 CPI 同比较上月下降 0.2pct，再次低于市场预期（0.8%）；CPI 环比持平，较上月回落 0.2pct，略
弱于季节性（0.12%）。分项来看，10月 CPI 内部分化再次加剧，食品项和非食品项同比增速差由-3.2%扩大
到-4.4%，食品项拖累加大。
剔除食品和能源后，10月核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，较上月回落 0.2pct。今年春节错位扰动后，核心 CPI
同比围绕中枢窄幅波动，本月触及区间低点。核心 CPI 的成色受到金价这一输入性因素影响，剔除相关的
其他用品及服务项后，10月核心 CPI 为 0.75%，略低于上月。
1、食品项：同比跌幅由 1.7 %扩大至 2.9%，环比由上涨 0.4%转为下跌 1.0%，显著弱于季节性（0.02%）。

（1）猪肉：供应充足叠加节后需求回落，猪价结束连续三个月上涨，10月环比转为下跌 1.0%，同比跌
幅收窄至 8.6%。猪价前瞻指标显示：9 月能繁母猪存栏量为 3602 万头，是正常保有量目标（3750 万头）
的 96%，仍在调控的绿色区间（92%-103%）内。生猪加速去产能，但传导仍存时滞，猪价短期上涨动能尚
不足。11月以来猪肉价格小幅回落。
（2）其他：今年迎来暖冬，鲜菜、鲜果、鸡蛋环比均由涨转跌。其中蔬菜供应随秋季上市增加，鲜菜
价格环比由上涨 0.5%转为下跌 5.8%，低于季节性（2.3%）8.1pct，同比降幅由 8.1%扩大到 16.9%，连续两
月是食品项的最主要拖累；鲜果环比由上涨 0.6%转为下跌 0.6%，同比跌幅由 1.5%扩大到 3.8%；节后备货
需求减少，蛋类环比由上涨 2.0%转为下跌 5.2%；休渔期结束，水产品供应充足，水产品价格环比下跌 1.3%，
跌幅扩大 0.5pct。
食品项同比跌幅扩大，鲜菜鲜果和蛋类环比同步转跌，拖累加大，秋季供应增加和天气改善是主因。

猪价受限于猪周期磨底及需求修复的滞后，短期难以转为 CPI 的正向拉动。
2、非食品项：同比上涨 1.5%，环比上涨 0.2%，同环比涨幅均持平上月，环比略强于季节性（0.12%）。
（1）能源和金属：美伊冲突反复，地缘风险溢价推升原油价格。根据统计局数据，汽油价格上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8pct，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63pct，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。
黄金面临加息压力和资金分流，价格继续回落，黄金饰品价格同比涨幅较上月收窄 18.0pct，影响 CPI 同比
上涨约 0.02pct，环比转为负向拖累。两项输入性因素产生双向拉动，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.11pct。

### Page 4

（2）服务：10月服务价格环比由下跌 0.2%转为上涨 0.1%，基本持平于季节性（0.04%），拉动项来自
假期出行需求和医疗调价。国庆假期出行人数增加，宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费分别上涨 6.5%、3.3%
和 2.4%，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04pct。政府主导的医疗调价继续提供支撑，医疗服务价格环比连续多
月上涨。
（3）AI 消费电子：涨价沿产业链继续传导，10 月通信工具 CPI 环比连续 11 个月上涨，同比上涨 16.1%，
再次刷新有数据以来的历史新高。数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格同比分别上涨 45.4%、
29.3%、23.0%和 16.7%，四项合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.26pct，较上月增加 0.03pct。
（4）补贴类商品：家用器具 CPI 环比连续 3 个月下跌，交通工具 CPI 环比连续 8个月下跌，居民大宗
商品价格竞争持续，汽车等部分行业产能过剩压力加大，持续向价格端传导。
（5）其他分项：毕业季后房屋进入租赁淡季，10 月房租 CPI 环比下跌 0.1%，弱于季节性（0.00%）。

房地产延续调整态势，贴息政策效果目前比较有限。
非食品中，主要支撑力量仍来自高油价和 AI 涨价两大外生性输入变量，内生需求延续低迷。服务价格
环比转涨，来自季节性出行需求和政府主导的医疗调价。家电、汽车等商品价格以下跌为主，耐用消费品
需求亟待提振。黄金同比回落，环比转为负向拖累。
10月终端商品与服务需求偏弱，基本对冲上游能源成本向下传导的动能，带动 CPI 同比回落。需求端，
短期制约有国补退坡、能源成本压力上升等因素，中长期面临居民预期低迷和资产负债表受损带来的“主
动去杠杆”。成本端传导并非普涨，而是延续结构性分化：国际油价支撑成品油，AI 产业链是主要亮点。

二、PPI：原油、煤炭和 AI 延续涨价主线 ，结构上出现三个变化
10 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，较前值上升 0.7pct，创近四年新高，再次强于市场预期；环比连续三个月上
涨，涨幅较上月回落 0.5pct至 0.7%。PPI 同比涨幅扩大，原油、煤炭和 AI 延续涨价主线；煤炭、石油相关
的能源化工链条对 PPI同比的拉动由 2.8pct扩大至 3.7pct，剔除能源链条后，PPI同比由 2.2%回落至 2.0%。
相比前月，PPI 结构上出现三个变化：
变化一是，油气链条的边际贡献由上游转移到中下游。油价直接拉动石油天然气开采、石油加工等上
游行业，并在产业链上沿着“原油→成品油/乙烯→塑料/橡胶/化纤→终端工业品”的路径传递，每环节传导

### Page 8

递减且后端环节存在时滞。10 月，上游的石油开采和加工价格环比继续上涨，但涨幅收窄，而中下游的化
工、化纤、橡胶塑料环比涨幅均扩大，对 PPI 的环比拉动增强。另外，中下游化工品的涨价幅度逐级衰减，
依次为化学原料（+ 3.1%）、化纤（+2.4%）、橡胶塑料（+0.7%），越靠近终端消费，涨价力度越弱。
变化二是，建材链条结构分化，形成地产拖累、基建托底。10 月，黑色金属和非金属价格形成显著背
离，其中黑色金属采选和加工环比增速均回落，上游采选价格跌幅扩大 0.4pct 至 1.7%；非金属采选和加工
价格环比上涨，且涨幅扩大。具体看细分品种，约七成需求绑定地产竣工的浮法玻璃价格下跌，反映房地产
需求延续弱势，而同时绑定地产和基建的水泥价格上涨，一方面来自供给收紧和成本上升，另一方面来自基
建项目施工需求改善。9 月以来稳投资政策密集落地，两重项目和“六张网”建设加快，对水泥等非金属建
材形成支撑。不过多数工业品价格下跌，基建项目的拉动效果属于托底范畴。
变化三是，AI 涨价持续，但是价格弹性收窄。10月，计算机通信电子制造业价格环比上涨 0.1%，近两

个月涨幅回落，连续三个季度的 AI 涨价压力有所缓解；有色金属加工价格环比转负，反映 AI 算力金属的
短期需求预期在降温。AI 链条已经从供给紧缺、涨价驱动的第一阶段，进入产能放量、更关注现金流和盈
利兑现能力的第二阶段，2027年资本支出增速的回落或影响其未来涨幅。
根据统计局测算，价格上涨的行业中，煤炭开采、油气开采、石油加工、化学原料和加工、燃气生产、
通信电子、有色采选、有色加工、电气制造这 9 个行业，合计影响 PPI 同比上涨 6.14pct。相比于 9 月统计
的 8 个上涨行业，本次纳入燃气生产和供应业，其 10月同比涨幅扩大 2.3pct 至 7.9%。同比下跌行业，主要
在中下游制造业，如汽车制造业、医药制造业、酒饮料和精制茶制造业，降幅在 1.6%-5.2%之间，但降幅较
上月有收窄。
累计口径显示，居民消费端温和上涨，工业成本端压力明显上升。1—10 月 CPI 累计同比为 0.9%，连
续四个月持平，2025年全年累计为 0.0%；1—10月 PPI 累计同比上涨 2.7%，较上月扩大 0.4pct，2025 年全

年为下降 2.6%。国内通胀延续“CPI 低位温和、PPI 走势偏强”的格局，CPI-PPI 剪刀差再次扩大。
利润层面，行业利润传导的裂口继续加大。沿着产业链价格同比涨幅看，10 月，PPI 采掘业（27.9%）
→原材料（11.6%）→加工（3.5%）→生活资料（-0.1%），采掘业到原材料增速差连续 9个月扩大，原材料
到加工环节的增速差连续 3 个月扩大，加工到生活资料的增速差相对稳定。上游资源品涨价，但是中下游企
业受制于激烈的市场竞争和偏弱的终端需求，产品提价困难，价格传导在中下游明显受阻，同时企业利润被
严重挤压，上中下游利润分化加剧。

三、展望和债市策略
（1）通胀趋势：核心 CPI 存回落压力，PPI 同比年内在 5%左右，2027年中枢回落，二季度或加速下
跌
对于 CPI，食品项重点分类中，猪周期继续磨底，年底腌腊需求有提振效果，鲜菜鲜果受秋季供应增加
的影响；厄尔尼诺背景下我国冬季整体可能偏暖，但阶段性寒潮频发，这种温差剧烈波动对蔬菜的伤害更
大，鲜菜 CPI 预计在冬季部分月份超季节性上涨。鲜菜在 CPI 中的权重是 1.7% ，若鲜菜价格同比涨幅扩大
10%，大约直接拉动整体 CPI 上涨 0.17 pct。非食品项中，四季度出行类服务需求进入淡季，汽车、家用器
具等耐用消费品受制于消费需求，通信工具和医疗服务有望继续支撑，能源项取决于原油走势。PPI 的结构
性上涨对 CPI 的拉动有限，同时在实体预期低迷、总需求没有明显改善的背景下，后续 CPI 同比预计在 0-
1%区间运行，核心 CPI 存回落压力。
对于 PPI，能源驱动特征明显。其中，原油价格受地缘因素主导，短期面临供应受阻以及北半球冬季取

暖需求支撑；特朗普长期战争能力被削弱，但在过渡期仍有能力制造可控的地缘紧张，以增加谈判筹码和稳
定美债利率，四季度原油或呈高位震荡走势。10 月中旬到 11 月中旬，油价均值环比上涨约 4.9%。国内煤
炭供给偏紧，叠加冬季用煤需求，价格或保持偏强走势。有色金属价格边际走弱，AI 需求预期短期降温。
国内定价的黑色金属、非金属等工业品价格分化，中长期仍面临房地产下行的制约，但财政资金加速投放、
“六张网”建设等提供支撑。综合上述因素，预计 11-12月 PPI 同比在 5%左右的高位，具体增幅主要取决
于油价中枢，但 PPI 环比涨速最快的时间或已经过去。2027年一季度开始，PPI 面临持续上升的基数，价

### Page 11

格中枢预计震荡回落，二季度回落速度或加快。
（2）一揽子增量政策效果观察：资金转化存堵点和时滞，实物工作量形成较慢
9 月以来，一揽子政策相继发力，稳投资部署持续落地，8000 亿新型政策性金融工具、5500 亿元结存
限额和专项债等资金投放进度加快，但建材链数据显示实物工作量对终端价格的提振效果有限。总结看，可
能来自两方面因素：一是项目端的施工进度成为主要约束，堵点正从“资金下达”转向“实物转化”；二是
政策资金的投向不偏向传统建材。8000亿新型政策性金融工具大部分投向人工智能、数字经济等新兴产业，
“六张网”里算力网、新型电网权重上升，单位投资用钢用水泥强度远低于传统“铁公基”。另外，政策资
金的实物转化需要时间，其中专项债从发行、拨付到形成实物量，常见时滞是 1-3 个月；用作资本金的新建
项目分化明显，对于手续、配套融资存在短板的项目，资本金到用材有 1-2 个季度时滞，部分项目甚至更长。
这意味着 9月底集中落地的专项债、PSL 等基建资金，若以 2个月中枢时滞测算，实物工作量、钢材水泥等

建材需求的增量窗口大概率落 11-12 月；如果是新建项目，兑现窗口可能会拖到明年一季度。用于项目全周
期的 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具，更大规模的投资拉动效应将在 2027年及之后逐步显现。
（3）对债市的影响：债市反应钝化，30年国债未来一年有望到 1.9%
10 月 PPI 再次超出预期，但并未改变利率下行趋势，主要在当前债市核心驱动来自政策宽松和信贷收
缩，对国内通胀数据基本钝化。
展望后续，短期信用债赎回风险快速修复，半年维度有经济压力增加、资金利率中枢下移、长端债券供
给减少、股市长周期调整等因素支撑，基本面和政策面决定债市中期仍处于顺风期，四季度债市可能出现
“抢跑”行情。30 年国债收益率按照预期向 2.0%靠近，未来一年有望到 1.9%。债市核心风险来自拥挤的交
易结构，重点关注央行态度、资金价格，以及股市可能再次下跌带来的赎回冲击，期间调整 3-5BP 或仍是加
仓机会。股市阶段底部较明确，11 月迎周度级别反弹，会修复短期风险偏好。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 09 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 期中選舉「藍色浪潮」，AI 基建政策風險升溫：11 月 3 日美國期中選舉民主黨奪回參眾兩院，
德州新任州長揚言暫停資料中心建設，市場擔憂電力、用水與環境審查趨嚴，AI 資本支出前景
遭重新評價；加上 Fed 官員釋出續升息訊號，資金撤出高評價半導體與 AI 基建族群，美股科
技股大幅修正。短線政策雜音難消，但 AI 需求本質未變，後續觀察新國會實際立法方向。
◼ 科技財報喜憂參半，台股賣壓沉重、政府出手穩市：蘋果營收優於預期但毛利率受記憶體成本

壓抑，AMD 資料中心營收近倍增、高通財報後走弱。美股賣壓延燒，台股連日重挫、新台幣
貶破 33 元，行政院指示金管會機動因應並研議穩定股市措施，官股行庫與投信持續買超。看
法：短線波動仍大，但基本面與政策支撐有助指數逐步築底。
◼ 美國製造業續擴張，台灣通膨降溫：美國 10 月 ISM 製造業指數升至 55.5，連續第十個月擴
張，惟價格分項仍處高檔；台灣 10 月製造業 PMI 連續 13 個月擴張但速度趨緩，CPI 年增率
降至 2.33%。看法：美國景氣與通膨黏性支撐 Fed 偏鷹立場，台灣央行壓力相對減輕；後續
關注 10 月非農就業、11 月 10 日美國 CPI 及 12 月 FOMC 會議。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，
惟短線宜重視個股分化，美國期中選舉帶來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，
惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，科技股整
體基本面仍具支撐，但短期應避免過度集中於高評價、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企

業。優先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業。
◼ 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價調整期
間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延
長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置
穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人
仍較為穩健，對 AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧；其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行，通膨風險則偏向上行，因此將保留政策彈性，根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明，強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，台股創高吸引外資匯入，推升台幣最高來到 31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至，台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市，而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局，主要在 6.7000 上方震盪，臨近周末，人行中間價為 6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來新低，不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至 4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔，市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存，美元指數維持高檔震盪，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險，並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震

壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並

本面與政策前景持續分歧下，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐，後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

2344 華邦電(Winbond)

報告日期：2026/11/06 研 究 員：王蕎程 (Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要 
 3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 為 8.06 元。 受惠記憶體
報價上漲與產品組合改善，第三季毛利率達 70.57%，預估第
四季營收 839.78 億元，QoQ +2.0%，毛利率 70.77%。 
 擴產與 16 奈米轉進帶動 2027 年位元出貨成長。 高雄廠
Module A 新增產能及台中廠 Flash 擴產陸續貢獻，搭配
DDR4、LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 需求，支持營運成長。 
 預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 23.29／37.45 元。 AI 伺服器與
企業級 SSD 需求為主要動能，CUBE 及矽電容拓展新應用。

一、3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 營收持續季增 
華邦電為利基型記憶體廠，產品涵蓋客製化記憶體、NOR Flash 及
SLC NAND。3Q26 營收 823.31 億元，QoQ +37.6%、YoY +278.2%；
營業毛利 581.01 億元，毛利率 70.57%；營業利益 437.84 億元，營
業利益率 53.18%；稅後純益 354.22 億元， EPS 7.58 元。營收結構
以客製化記憶體占 58%、Flash 占 32%、邏輯 IC 占 9%。其中，客
製化記憶體平均售價季增近 60%，Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，為
第三季成長主因。展望第四季，公司表示 AI 需求維持強勁，部分
PC 與消費性客戶調整拉貨，整體營運仍將成長，惟增幅低於前幾
季。考量合約價格續升及產品組合改善，預估 4Q26 營收 839.78 億
元，QoQ +2.0%；毛利率 70.77%，營業利益 448.28 億元，稅後 EPS 
8.06 元。

二、高雄廠擴產，支應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 長期需求 
公司持續供應 DDR3、DDR4 及 LPDDR4，受惠主要記憶體原廠調
整產品配置，以及車用、工業客戶產品生命週期長、替換供應來
源不易，長期供貨需求具支撐。高雄廠 Module A 月產能預計由約
1.5 萬片提高至 2.4 萬至 2.5 萬片，新增產能於 2027 年陸續貢獻，
並提高 16 奈米製程比重，同時增加晶圓投入與單片晶圓位元產
出。法人看好擴產搭配製程轉進可帶動 2027 年 DRAM 位元出貨顯
著成長，中信預估增幅超過 100%。本報告採新增產能與製程效益
逐步反映的假設，預估 2027 年營收逐季成長。長期方面，董事會
通過 731 億元資本支出，用於高雄廠 Module B 土建、機電及長交
期設備，規劃月產能約 5 萬至 6 萬片，預計 2029 年下半年開始小
量出貨，並以長期供貨協議支應策略客戶需求。

三、AI 帶動 CUBE 與矽電容拓展新應用 
AI 伺服器機櫃內 GPU、BMC、SmartNIC 及交換器等元件均需 Flash
儲存韌體與設定資料，隨運算及管理架構複雜化，帶動使用顆數
與容量提升。公司預估供應 AI 生態系的 Flash 位元出貨量於 2026
年成長 112%，2027 年再增 67%，其中高容量 NOR Flash 為主要動
能；企業級 SSD 亦增加 DRAM 需求，擴大公司在伺服器供應鏈的
參與。法人看好台中廠擴產與高容量產品組合改善。新產品方面，
CUBE 主要布局 AI 加速器與推論應用，預計 2027 年下半年增加出
貨；矽電容已小量出貨，既有產能多獲客戶預訂，並透過代工模
式切入 AI 晶片、先進封裝及高速光模組應用，逐步增加產品附加
價值與長期營收來源。 
四. 投資建議：買進，目標價 180 元 
華邦電為全球最大 NOR Flash 廠亦是利基型 DRAM 廠，AI 帶動各
式記憶體需求增加，產能仍供不應求下，獲利將逐季成長。公司
針對邊緣 AI 發展之 CUBE 解決方案將於 2027 年開始貢獻，矽電容
亦為新的業務機會，中長期而言 AI、HPC、IoT、自駕車等新產品
新應用將帶動更多記憶體需求。考量到 1) AI 帶動之記憶體供不應
求將延續至 2028 年、2)華邦電今明年營收獲利創高、3)CUBE、矽
電容具成長潛力，參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 180
元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
華邦電子於 1987 年九月由華新麗華集團轉投資成立，為自有品牌
IC 設計及製造廠商，並為全世界第五大 DRAM 供應商，且為國內
少數擁有晶圓廠之積體電路設計公司，同時也是全球利基型記憶
體的主要供應商。2008 年公司將邏輯 IC 事業分割成立新唐科技，
新唐產品線以消費性電子 IC、電腦 IC 兩大產品線為主，並同時擁
有一座 6 吋晶圓廠。華邦電在 NOR Flash 為全球最大供應商，產
品上從中低容量市場拓展至中高容量市場。NOR Flash 主要同業為
旺宏、兆易創新、Infineon。

二.2026 年營運概況及展望

1. 2026 下半年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 2026 年 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 下半年，預估記憶體求維持強勁下，價格持續上漲，唯
因價格基期已高，加上傳統消費性產品追價力道有限，漲幅不若
上半年，不過預估今年隨著價格上漲，加上製程轉換帶動位元出

貨量增加，及新產能貢獻，營收逐季成長，毛利率持續提升，預
估華邦電 2026 年營收 2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，
稅後淨利 1,047.92 億元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

3. 2027 年記憶體更加緊缺，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 帶動之記憶體缺貨狀況延續，加上全球新增產能
有限，緊缺情況更加嚴峻，而儘管終端電子產品需求不一，不過
未來 AI 落地後每單位產品之記憶體搭載量持續成長，使得記憶體
位元出貨提升，2027 年公司營運仍正向。DDR4 在全球三大廠逐步
退出下價格自 2Q25 起漲，且因終端電子產品仍有一定需求，價格
將維持高檔，NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 亦因供給端增產有限，價格
逐步回升，其中 SLC Nand 因大廠退出，華邦將成為全球最大供應
商，而應用於 AI Server 的高容量 NOR Flash 持續供不應求，預期
將推升價格上行至 2027 年。華邦電製程技術方面，20 奈米 DRAM
產品仍為主要出貨主力，應用於 DDR3、DDR4、LPDDR 產品，16
奈米製程已開發完成，2027 年將有一定比例轉換至 16 奈米；NOR 
Flash 營收穩定成長，NAND Flash 24 奈米及 32 奈米 SLC Nand 今年
2Q 貢獻顯著提升。因應記憶體需求強勁，公司 Flash 與 DRAM 擴
產計畫進行中，華邦電 2025 年資本支出為台幣 55 億元，2026 年
將提升至 395 億元，其中生產設備資本支出約占 95%，大部分用
於高雄產能及製程提升，少部分用於台中廠製程提升，高雄廠產
能將由現在 15k 擴充至年底的 24k，屆時位元出貨量可望翻倍，同
時啟動高雄 Module B 投資計劃，規劃月產能可達 50k~60k，將分
階段擴充，預估 2027 年 1 月動工，2029 年 1 月裝機，2029 年底量
產。2027 年預估 DRAM、NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 價格持續上漲帶
動營收獲利成長，預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛
利率 72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

4. CUBE 及矽電容為新成長動能 
AI 時代來臨，華邦電長期專注於利基型 DRAM，針對 AI 將主攻邊
緣運算市場，邊緣運算之記憶體容量不需像 AI Server 那麼高，為
華邦電可著墨之區塊，因此華邦電提供 CUBE(Customized Ultra 
Bandwidth Element 客製化超高頻寬記憶體元件)解決方案，CUBE
具備超低功耗和超高性能、小尺寸、高成本效益，從而大幅提升
系統能力、回應時間和能源效率，以滿足邊緣運算平臺上快速增
長的 AI 應用需求。CUBE 優點在於 1)SoC 推疊在上面，散熱效果
較好、2) 可在 CUBE Die 上做穿孔(TSV)，降低成本、3) 3D 推疊
適合較小的終端裝置。目前華邦電與多家客戶開發 CUBE 方案，
預估 2026 年底開始貢獻，2027 年放量。另外因應 AI 晶片功耗劇
增、瞬間電流波動，以及先進封裝的空間限制，矽電容具有極低
的等效串聯電感與寄生電感，能在極短時間內補償電流，維持電

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 3Q26 毛利率升至 70.57%，EPS 7.58 元 
華邦電 3Q26 營收 823.31 億元(QoQ+37.6%、YoY+278.2%)，其中記
憶體事業營收 742.84 億元(QoQ+43.7%)。受惠客製化記憶體平均售
價季增近 60%、Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，合併毛利率提升至
70.57%，營業利益 437.84 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 354.22 億元，
EPS 7.58 元。展望第四季，記憶體合約價格持續上升，惟部分 PC
與消費性客戶調整拉貨，預估營收 839.78 億元(QoQ+2.0%)，毛利
率 70.77%，EPS 8.06 元。

二. 2026 年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 年下半年，AI 伺服器需求強勁，帶動 DDR4、LPDDR4
及高容量 Flash 價格持續上漲，惟第四季部分 PC 與消費性客戶調
整拉貨，營運成長幅度較第三季收斂。預估華邦電 2026 年營收
2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，稅後淨利 1,047.92 億
元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

三. 2027 年擴產與製程轉進帶動成長，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 伺服器與企業級 SSD 需求持續成長，DDR4、
LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 供需維持緊俏，加上高雄廠新增產能
與 16 奈米製程轉進，帶動位元出貨增加，CUBE 及矽電容亦逐步
貢獻營收。預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛利率
72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

### Page 1

Advanced Energy Solution (6781.TW)
Growth Intact Despite A Longer HVDC Transition
CITI'S TAKE 
We walked away from AES’s 3Q briefing more confident in the BBU growth 
outlook. Resilient low-voltage demand and higher-power product 
upgrades should sustain growth through the HVDC transition, with 
400V/800V commercialization targeted for mid/end-2027E respectively. 
Rising technical complexity should favor AES’s engineering expertise and 
established customer relationships. We trim our TP to NT$1,550 (from 
NT$1,680) on 25x 2027E P/E from 27x reflecting HVDC timing uncertainty. 
Reiterate Buy as rising BBU penetration and content per rack underpin 
structural growth.
3Q26: strong BBU demand drives growth and mix upgrades lift GPM y/y — 3Q 
sales of NT$5.2bn rose 27% y/y and 9% q/q, driven by strong BBU demand and were 
9% above our forecast. GPM expanded 1.3ppt y/y to 36.3% on mix upgrades, though 
it fell 2.8ppt q/q. Op profits rose 34% y/y with OPM up 1.4ppt y/y to 24.6%. Net profit 
of NT$1.1bn was up 34% y/y, 3% above our forecast but 5% below consensus. Higher 
opex and lower non-op income partly offset the sales beat.
Low voltage BBUs retain a substantial growth runway — Mgmt expects steady 4Q 
growth and remains positive on 2027E, with no downward revisions to customer 
demand plans. Continued datacenter buildout should sustain low-voltage BBU 
procurement while HVDC architectures undergo qualifications. Demand for 3kW 
products remains broad across conventional servers and lower power datacenter 
workloads. AES is working with several new CSPs and overseas SIs, with new 
customer contributions expected in 2027E. Rising GPU power, greater BBU 
penetration and higher content per MW should support volumes and mix-led ASP 
growth. Cell prices are rising, but AES indicates stable procurement through long-
standing relationships with suppliers and will seek to pass on cost increases. 
HVDC progress: commercialization likely in 2H27E — High voltage BBU 
commercialization has been delayed to 2H27E, reflecting product development and 
system integration challenges. Currently samples have been delivered and large-
scale testing is underway for 400V products, with mass production targeted for 
mid-2027E and 800V products are scheduled for end-2027E. Mgmt. sees high 
voltage BBU ASP to be 5-10x higher than low voltage products. We believe 
increasing system complexity and qualification requirements favor AES’s 
established engineering capabilities and CSP relationships, though shipment 
timing and margin contribution require monitoring.
n
Angela Hsu AC

### Page 5

Advanced Energy Solution (6781.TW)
09 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Advanced Energy Solution
Company description
Established in January 2020 and listed in March 2021, Advanced Energy
Solution Holdings (AES) is a holding company registered in Cayman Island,
supplying battery packs for LEV (light electrical vehicle such as e-Bikes) and
BBU (battery back-up unit for datacenters) applications. Prior to the
establishment and IPO, AES was a BU focusing on non-IT applications within

its parent company, Simplo Technology (6121.TW).
Investment strategy
We rate AES shares as Buy. Being an industry leader in battery pack design
and manufacturing with a proven track record, we expect AES to benefit from
higher BBU content per rack and deep CSP engagement as the industry
transitions from conventional 48V systems to HVDC architecture. BBU now
contributes >70% of sales and should remain the key earnings driver as AI
rack power continues to rise. We forecast earnings CAGR of 27% in 2025-

2028E, supported by strong BBU demand, mix upgrade toward high-power
BBU products and op leverage.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$1,550 is based on 25x 2027E EPS on par with its
historical average since listing and supported by a 2025-28E earnings CAGR
of 27%. We use 2027E as the year of valuation base as we think 2027E can
better reflect its earnings power after the commercializarion of HVDC
products.
Risks
Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts that could prevent the shares

from reaching our target price include: 1) slower-than-expected end demand
from e-Bikes post the pandemic and volaitlity in cloud capex deployment that
could impact datacenter BBU sales; 2) component supply
constraints/logistics challenges, which could lead to slower-than-expected
shipment and top-line momentum; and 3) peer competition, which could
impact sales growth and margins negatively, in our view.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Update
Advantech  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +5% MoM/+88%
YoY
In this report, we focus on Advantech's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales were NT$11,336mn (+5% MoM/+88% YoY).
• QTD revenue reached 35% of MSe, at NT$32,600mn (flat QoQ/+82% YoY),
and 37% of consensus, at NT$30,364mn (-6% QoQ/+69% YoY).
• By region, North America (+118%) and Korea (+112%) outperformed; other
markets, including Europe (+56%), China (+95%), Taiwan (+70%), Japan

(+44%) and emerging markets (+22%) also performed strongly.
• By segment, Intelligent Systems was +82% YoY,   IoT Automation was +24%
YoY,  Intelligent Service was +27% YoY. The Embedded segment grew 91%
YoY, within which Embedded IoT grew 124% YoY, while Applied Computing
grew 53% YoY for the month.
Our view:
• We think its 4Q26 revenue is tracking to be up QoQ, consistent with
management comments during the latest earnings call.
• Backed by the solid B/B ratio of 1.60 in October (vs. 1.64 in 3Q26), we expect

the revenue momentum to sustain into the coming quarters.
• We remain constructive on Advantech. It is well positioned to benefit from
accelerating IoT market growth amid edge AI proliferation.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Advantech (2395.TW)
Base case, 26x 2027e P/E. We view our target multiple as justified by our 36% earnings CAGR
forecast for 2025-28. It implies a PEG of 0.7, vs. the stock's average one-year forward P/E of
25x in 2015-22 and the earnings CAGR of 8% during that period, with a PEG of 3.5.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected macroeconomic conditions
n More top- and bottom-line growth from the platform and applications

Risks to Downside
n Weaker macro environment
n Slower progress in edge AI implementation

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

M
Idea
Wiwynn Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q beat on higher GM/OpM
Recent concerns revolve around potential data center delays,
though the supply chain has not seen any changes. However, we
lower our mid-term growth  rate to bake in some conservatism.
Our PT now implies a lower CY27 P/E at  12x. RR looks attractive
at 7.7x CY27 P/E vs. the 3Y  avg. of ~10x.
4Q revenue should continue to grow double-digits q/q: Wiwynn announced
October revenue at ~NT$165B (-4% m/m, +81% y/y). If we assume the October

monthly run-rate can be sustained, 4Q should grow at least 13-14% q/q. However,
we think ASIC server racks will continue to ramp-up through-out year-end, so we
forecast 4Q revenue to ~20% q/q to reach ~NT$520B (+78% y/y). Higher cost
structure and higher mix of ASIC server racks should drive margins to decline q/q to
~8.5% (-40bps q/q), resulting in net profit of ~NT$27.7B or EPS of NT$46.70.
ASIC/GPU projects remain on track: Our checks indicate that  Trainium3 server rack

production is ramping up well at Wiwynn, and with T3, Wiwynn is a more
comprehensive partner, also engaging in UBB and switch trays. It will also engage in
AMD's (covered by Joe Moore) MI455 Helios rack assembly for customers such as
Meta (covered by Brian Nowak) and Oracle (covered by Sanjit Singh). We forecast
Wiwynn to build ~8K Helios racks for CY27.
3Q results beat on better margins: Revenue was NT$434.6B (+63% y/y) with GM
of 8.9% (-40bps q/q), which is 90bps/30bps above MSe/Street. OP reached ~NT

$32.5B (+66% y/y) for OpM of 7.5% (+20bps q/q), which is 100bps/50bps above
MSe/Street, driven by operating leverage. Non-op losses were slightly higher than
MSe/Street mainly due to higher interest expense, resulting in net profit of ~NT
$23.4B (+52% y/y) for EPS of NT$42.09, 44%/10% above MSe/Street.
Reiterate OW with price target unchanged at NT$2,515 (12x/11x CY27/28e P/E) –
our higher EPS estimates are offset by a lower mid-term growth rate. Risk-reward is

highly attractive at 7.7x CY27e or 6.7x CY28e P/E vs. the three-year range of 6-16x,
with a strong pipeline of AI and non-AI server projects.

### Page 6

Idea
6
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
We keep our price target unchanged at NT$2,515: This implies 12x CY27e P/E or 11x CY28
P/E, vs. its thee-year average forward P/E of 10.3x, which we think is fair, in view of
Wiwynn's AI server project wins and share gains for general-purpose servers in the next 1-
2 years.
We continue to use a residual income model to derive our price target. Key assumptions
include a cost of equity of 7.0% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 6% and risk-free rate of

1%), a medium-term growth rate of 4% (lowered from 6%), and a terminal growth rate of
3%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$3,210 and NT$1,470,
respectively.

by rising demand for agentic AI.

contribute in CY27.

12x 2027e P/E

servers to account for ~50% of revenue in
2027, from 20-30% in 2024, as Wiwynn
ramps up its AI server shipments and
general server demand also grows strongly.
NT$1,470.00
7.3x 2027e P/E
Data center builds get pushed out: Assumes
geopolitical tensions escalate, with macro
data deteriorating, driving hyperscale

vendors such as Meta and Microsoft to push
out their data center builds, which leads to
further demand downside for both general
computing servers and AI servers.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

Giant Manufacturing

3Q26 EPS of NT$0.85 missed on margins

Maintain Rating: UNDERPERFORM | PO: 87.00 TWD | Price: 88.00 TWD

3Q26 EPS of NT$0.85 missed on margins  
Giant reported 3Q26 EPS of NT$0.85 (-4% YoY), which missed our estimate of NT$1.16 
on margins. The miss was due to higher than expected SG&A expenses as we believe the 
company continues to invest in marketing to support its new product launches as well as 
legal fees related to the WRO issues. 3Q26 revenue was +6% YoY to NT$16.3bn, which 
was in-line with preliminary revenue. 3Q26 gross margin was +2.4pps YoY to 23.9%. 
OPM was +0.5pps YoY to 3.1%.  
Oct revenue was up 2% YoY  
Oct revenue was up by 2% YoY. Growth in OB revenue was offset by lower OE 
contribution. Within OB business, Europe turned to a single digit decline while China 
achieved double digit growth. US reported steep decline due to weak market demand 
and negative impact from WRO investigation. We expect revenue growth to remain 
unexciting in 4Q26 as there should be limited product launches to drive sales during the 
winter season.  
Cut PO to N$87; Maintain Underperform  
We cut 2027E EPS by 7% to reflect 3Q26 miss. Based on unchanged 18x 2027E P/E, we 
cut our PO by 7% to NT$87. We expect end demand to remain under pressure due to 
macro uncertainty and inflation. We see downside risks to VA consensus EPS growth of 
83% YoY to NT$6.1 for 2027E given uncertainty in sales recovery and margins 
normalization.

Company Sector 
Leisure

Company Description 
Established in 1972, Giant is the largest global bicycle 
manufacturer. Giant operates its own brand business 
(OBM) (68% of revenue) under Giant (mid-to-high-end 
bikes), Liv (women's), and Momentum (low-end) brand 
names. Giant's largest OEM customers are Trek (16% of 
revenue) and Scott (13% of revenue). Giant generated 39% 
of revenue from Europe, 23% from North America, 12% 
from China and 26% from Others in 2020. Its production is 
mainly based in China and Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We rate Giant shares Underperform. We see high 
uncertainty in earnings recovery given weak end demand 
and negative earnings impact from FX/tariff headwinds.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 1.0x

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Giant Manufacturing (GTMUF) 
Our NT$87 price objective is based on 18x 2027E P/E. The target multiple of 18x is at 
0.5SD below the long-term average of 25x (2011-2025) given higher macro uncertainty 
and slow earnings growth.

Downside risks to our price objective are COVID resurgence to further disrupt supply 
chain and unexpectedly cool-down in bike demand. 
Upside risks to our price objective are stronger-than-expected demand, fast capacity 
expansion and efficiency ramp-up, more favorable input costs and product mix shift.

Amortization 
Return On Equity Net Income Shareholders’ Equity 
Operating Margin Operating Profit Sales 
Earnings Growth Expected 5 Year CAGR From Latest Actual N/A 
Free Cash Flow Cash Flow From Operations − Total Capex N/A  
Quality of Earnings Numerator Denominator 
Cash Realization Ratio Cash Flow From Operations Net Income 
Asset Replacement Ratio Capex Depreciation 
Tax Rate Tax Charge Pre-Tax Income 
Net Debt-To-Equity Ratio Net Debt = Total Debt − Cash & Equivalents Total Equity 
Interest Cover EBIT Interest Expense

Price / Earnings Ratio Current Share Price Diluted Earnings Per Share (Basis As Specified) 
Price / Book Value Current Share Price Shareholders’ Equity / Current Basic Shares 
Dividend Yield Annualised Declared Cash Dividend Current Share Price 
Free Cash Flow Yield Cash Flow From Operations − Total Capex Market Cap = Current Share Price × Current Basic Shares 
Enterprise Value / Sales EV = Current Share Price × Current Shares + Minority Equity + Net Debt +

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating) 
Ratings dispersion guidelines for coverage clusterR2 
Buy ≥ 10% ≤ 70% 
Neutral ≥ 0% ≤ 30% 
Underperform N/A ≥ 20% 
R2Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster. 
INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

### Page 2

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 8% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 1%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 9%/8% to reflect product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 6/6%). We now expect the GM expansion 
to be slower due to the higher server business contribution that is margin dilutive, thus 
revise down GPM by 0.2/0.2/0.2ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation:  
We reiterate our Neutral rating on Inventec with a new 12m TP of NT$56 (from NT$61), 
based on 14.2x 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E for the past 
10 years, and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Inventec is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, servers, 
and consumer electronics. While we like Inventec’s solid position in the AI server ODM 
market, we are Neutral rated as we believe valuation already reflects near-term 
positives. We model Inventec earnings growth in 2026-28E to be driven by the 
commercial PC demand recovery and AI server ramp-up. We model AI servers to 
contribute more in 2026-28E. We see P/E valuation as fair at the current level compared 
to its peers and already reflects the solid growth in 2026-28E. Catalysts for further 
upside/downside would be its customers’ demand on AI servers and general server 
demand outlook.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$56 is based on 14.2x 2027E P/E, which is 
the company’s average P/E for the past 10 years. 
Key upside/downside risks: (1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
(2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and (3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

Document 303: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-14).pdf
Report source: Unknown

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

Document 348: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP, household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP. The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP, is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

for a changing
world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

上漲，預計4Q26每月經常性收入成長10~11%，營

收成長12-13%，調整後EBITDA利潤率季節性降至
49-50%，預估4Q26調整後FFO約YOY+9%，雖看
好，但易受市場對AI融資及變現疑慮壓抑估值擴張。

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年11 月 11 日

1/5 
インドネシア、ギブラン副大統領の辞任意向表明で政局に新たな波乱 
～プラボウォ・ジョコ両氏の関係変化を背景に、2029 年に向けた政治勢力再編が進む可能性～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インドネシアでは、ギブラン副大統領がプラボウォ大統領に辞任の意向を伝えたことが明ら
かになり、政局に新たな動揺が広がっている。背景には、2024 年の大統領選挙に際して提出
した学歴証明書類の有効性を巡り、司法機関が相次いで同氏に不利な判断を示したことがあ
る。議会でも資格問題を追及する動きが強まるなか、ギブラン氏は法的手段による対応を模
索する一方、問題が政権全体を巻き込む政治的混乱に発展することを回避すべく、辞任の意
向を示したと考えられる。ただし、現時点では正式な辞任には至っていない。 
○ 今回の問題の背景には、プラボウォ大統領とジョコ前大統領の政治的関係の変化が影響して
いる可能性もある。両者は 2024 年の大統領選挙を通じて協力関係を構築したものの、プラボ
ウォ政権の発足後は、閣僚人事や新首都建設を巡る政策面での違いなどから、次第に距離が
生じてきたとされる。さらに、足元ではジョコ氏に近い人物の拘束や、同氏の次男が率いる
政党からの閣僚らの離党が相次ぎ、両者の政治的力学に変化が生じている可能性がある。 
○ ギブラン氏が正式に辞任した場合、後任の副大統領の選定を通じて、プラボウォ政権内部の
勢力関係が変化する可能性がある。幅広い政党による連立体制を背景に、辞任が直ちに政権
の存続を脅かすとは限らず、むしろ後任人事を通じて政権基盤を強化する余地もある。他方、
与党連立内部の利害調整が複雑化する可能性があるほか、ジョコ氏にとっては現政権への重
要な影響力行使の手段を失うことにつながるため、2029 年の次期大統領・議会選挙を見据え
た政治勢力の再編が進むことも考えられる。 
○ 一方、インドネシア金融市場では、中銀の独立性や財政規律への懸念などを背景に、通貨ル
ピアや株式相場への下押し圧力がくすぶっている。こうしたなかで政治的混乱が長期化すれ
ば、政策運営の予見可能性に対する懸念を通じて金融市場の新たな重しとなる可能性がある。
先行きは、ギブラン氏の正式な辞任の有無に加え、プラボウォ政権がジョコ前政権からの政
治的な自立をどこまで進めるのか、そして 2029 年に向けて主要政治勢力の関係がどのよう
に変化するかが重要な焦点となる。

### Page 2

2/5 
【ギブラン副大統領が突然の辞任意向を表明、立候補資格を巡る問題の再燃が背景に】 
インドネシアの政局が新たな動揺に見舞われている。同国政府は 10 日、ギブラン副大統領がプ
ラボウォ大統領と約２時間半にわたって会談し、副大統領職を辞任する意向を伝えたことを明らか
にした。政府側は、プラボウォ氏が辞任の意向を拒否しなかったと説明している。背景には、2024
年の大統領選挙に際して提出した学歴証明書類の有効性を巡り、司法機関が相次いで同氏に不利な
判断を示したことがある。なお、ギブラン氏は辞任の意向を伝える一方、行政裁判所での手続きを
通じて自身の学歴証明書類の有効性を立証する機会を求めているとされる。プラボウォ氏も法的手
段による対応を助言したとされ、現時点では正式な辞任には至っていない。このため、ギブラン氏
は引き続き副大統領として職務を遂行しているものの、今回の動きは、2024 年の大統領選挙を通じ
て形成されたプラボウォ氏とジョコ前大統領の政治的協力関係に変化が生じている可能性を示唆
している。 
今回、ギブラン氏による辞任意向表明に至った背景には、同氏の副大統領への立候補資格を巡る
問題がある。同氏は、2024 年の大統領選挙への出馬に際して、選挙法で定める大統領、副大統領候
補の年齢規定（40 歳以上）を下回る 36 歳であったにもかかわらず、憲法裁判所が地方自治体の首
長経験者について立候補年齢の例外を認めたことで、副大統領候補となることが可能になった。こ
の判断を巡っては、当時の憲法裁判所長官であったアンワル氏がジョコ前大統領の義弟（妹の夫）
に当たるなど利益相反が問題視され、その後、同氏は重大な倫理違反を理由に長官職を解かれた経
緯がある（注１）。しかし、その後も司法判断そのものは無効とはされず、ギブラン氏はプラボウォ
氏とともに大統領選挙に勝利した。このため、同氏の立候補を巡っては、当初から政治的な論争が
続いてきたものの、足元では年齢要件とは別に、学歴要件を巡る問題が浮上している。 
具体的には、10 月６日に憲法裁判所が学歴要件を巡る申し立てを原告適格の問題から不受理と
した一方、ギブラン氏が海外（シンガポールのオーキッド・パーク中等学校、オーストラリアのシ
ドニー工科大学付属インサーチ）で受けた教育について、国内の高校卒業資格に相当することを裏
付ける十分な証拠が確認できないとの見解を示した。さらに、10 月 30 日には最高裁判所が、2024
年の立候補に際して提出された高校卒業資格の同等性を認める証明書を無効とする判断を下した。
こうした司法判断を受けて、議会でもギブラン氏の資格問題を追及する動きが強まっている。国民
代表議会（下院）では、６会派に所属する約 50 人の議員が、弾劾手続きにつながり得る「意見表明
権」の行使を提案したとされる。このため、ギブラン氏としては、法的な争いを継続する姿勢を維
持する一方、自身の進退問題が政権全体を巻き込む政治的混乱に発展することを回避すべく、辞任
の意向を示したと考えられる。

【政局の底流には、プラボウォ大統領とジョコ前大統領の関係悪化懸念が影響している可能性も】 
今回の問題を巡っては、ギブラン氏個人の立候補資格だけでは説明し切れない側面がある。プラ

注１ 2023 年 11 月８日付レポート「インドネシア大統領選､ジョコ氏長男を｢後押し｣した憲法裁所長に辞任命令」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/288141.html）

### Page 3

3/5 
ボウォ氏とジョコ前大統領は、2014 年と 2019 年の大統領選挙では対立候補として激戦を演じた。
しかし、2019 年に発足したジョコ政権２期目では、プラボウォ氏が国防相に登用されたことをきっ
かけに、両者の関係が改善に転じた。さらに、2024 年の大統領選挙では、プラボウォ氏がジョコ氏
の長男であるギブラン氏を副大統領候補に迎え、ジョコ政権の政策を継承する姿勢を示したことで、
ジョコ氏の支持基盤を取り込むことに成功した。このため、プラボウォ政権の発足当初においては、
両者の政治的協力関係が政権基盤の安定化を図る重要な要素となっていた。しかし、政権発足から
時間が経過するにつれ、プラボウォ氏が独自の政策運営を強める一方、内閣改造が実施されるたび
にジョコ前政権の関係者を要職から外す動きがみられた。その結果、両者の関係には次第に距離が
生じてきたとされる。さらに、プラボウォ政権下では、ジョコ前大統領が推進した新首都ヌサンタ
ラの建設予算が抑制されるなど、政策面でも優先順位の違いが表面化している。 
足元では、両者の関係を一段と複雑する動きが顕在化している。国家警察は 11 月初め、ジョコ
氏に近いとされるリスティオ大統領前首席補佐官（前国家警察長官）らを、プラボウォ氏を含む政
府高官などへの違法な通信傍受に関与した疑いで拘束した。この問題を巡っては捜査が続いており、
ジョコ氏などの関与が確認されている訳ではないものの、両者の政治的関係を巡る警戒感を高める
一因となっている。さらに、直後には、ジョコ氏の次男（カエサン氏）が党首を務めるインドネシ
ア連帯党（ＰＳＩ）に所属する現職閣僚や副大臣３名が相次いで離党を表明した。３名は離党の理
由に、プラボウォ政権での職務の専念などを挙げたものの、一連の動きは、政権関係者の間でジョ
コ氏との政治的な距離を見直す動きが生じている可能性を示唆している。その意味では、今回のギ
ブラン氏の辞任意向表明についても、こうした政治的な力学の変化を踏まえて捉える必要がある。

【副大統領辞任なら政権基盤の再編が進む可能性、ジョコ前大統領の動きにも注意】 
今後、ギブラン氏が正式に副大統領職を辞任すれば、プラボウォ氏による政権運営にも一定の変
化が生じると考えられる。現行憲法では、副大統領に欠員が生じた場合、大統領が推薦する２人の
候補者から、国民協議会（国民代表議会と地方代表議会で構成）が 60 日以内に新たな副大統領を
選出すると定められている（第８条第２
項）。このため、ギブラン氏の辞任後は、
後任の選定を巡って与党連立内の調整
が必要となる。プラボウォ政権は幅広い
政党を取り込んだ連立体制を構築して
いるため（図１）、副大統領の辞任が直ち
に政権の存続を脅かすとは限らない。む
しろ、後任人事を通じて大統領の政策運
営に協力的な勢力との結び付きを強め
ることも可能となる。ただし、主要政党
が後任ポストや政策面での影響力拡大
を求めれば、連立内部の利害調整がこれまで以上に複雑化することも予想される。加えて、司法判
図 1 国民議会における各党の議席数

（出所）各種報道より作成, 太字は与党, 斜字は閣外協力

### Page 4

4/5 
断や議会による追及への対応が長期化する場合には、政権による政策遂行に対する政治的な負担が
高まることも考えられる。 
一方で、今回の動きはジョコ前大統領の政治的な立場にも影響を及ぼすと考えられる。同氏は大
統領退任後も高い知名度と一定の支持基盤を維持しており、近年はカエサン氏が率いるＰＳＩを通
じて政治的な活動を活発化させてきた。しかし、ギブラン氏が副大統領を退任すれば、ジョコ氏が
現政権に対して影響力を行使するうえで重要な接点が失われる。他方、ジョコ氏が引き続きＰＳＩ
や自身の支持者組織を通じて政治活動を続ける場合、政権との協力関係を再構築する動きが生じる
可能性があるものの、現政権とは異なる政治的な立場を鮮明にする展開も考えられる。とりわけ、
2029 年に実施予定の次期大統領・議会選挙に向けて、プラボウォ氏を支える既存の与党勢力とジョ
コ氏に近い勢力がどのような関係を築くかは、政治的な争点のひとつになる。ジョコ氏の影響力の
行方を判断するうえでは、ギブラン氏自身の政治活動の継続に加え、ＰＳＩの組織基盤の拡大や既
存政党との協力関係が重要となる。

【政治的混乱が金融市場の新たな重しとなる可能性、2029 年に向けた政局再編にも注目】 
今回の政治的混乱によって、金融市場が大幅な動揺に見舞われるかは不透明である。とはいえ、
足元のインドネシア市場を巡っては、中銀の独立性を巡る懸念などを理由に通貨ルピア安圧力がく
すぶるとともに、指数算出会社のＭＳＣＩが同国を「スタンドアローン市場」に再分類する案につ
いて協議を開始したことなどを受けて
（注２）、その後の主要株価指数（ジャカ
ルタ総合指数）は調整の動きを強めてい
る（図２）。さらに、プラボウォ政権が推
進する無償給食事業などの大幅な財政
出動による悪影響への懸念も、市場の重
しとなっている。こうした状況のなかで
政治的な混乱が長期化すれば、政策運営
の予見可能性や財政規律に対する投資
家の評価を通じて、金融市場に影響が及
ぶ可能性は否定できない。とりわけ、与
党連立内部における対立が予算編成や政権による政策遂行に波及するか否かが重要になる。 
先行きについては、ギブラン氏が司法手続きを通じて自身の立場を維持する場合と、正式な辞任
に踏み切り、新たな副大統領の選出が進む場合の双方を念頭に置く必要がある。前者の場合は、資
格問題を巡る政治的な対立が長期化する可能性があり、後者の場合は、副大統領の後任人事を通じ
て政権内部の勢力関係が変化することが考えられる。ただし、いずれの展開となった場合でも、プ

5/5
ラボウォ氏とジョコ前大統領との関係が完全な決裂に向かうとは限らず、政治的な利害に応じて協
力関係が維持・再構築される余地も残されている。今回の辞任意向表明を巡っては、正式な辞任に
至るか否かだけでなく、プラボウォ政権がジョコ前政権からの政治的な自立をどこまで進めるのか、
そして 2029 年に向けてインドネシアの主要政治勢力の関係がどのように変化するかが、今後の政
局を見通していくうえで重要な焦点になると考えられる。

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー
